ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
WinWay Technology
Final point of contact: securing AI yield through
advanced testing socket
Solid growth ahead with robust capacity expansions
We initiate coverage of AI/HPC test socket leader WinWay with a Buy rating and NT
$8,900 price target. WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak
due to broader market de-rating and margin pressure associated with its Technoprobe

partnership and capacity expansion. We believe the recent correction presents an
opportunity to re-engage with WinWay's long-term growth trajectory, supported by
three key drivers: 1) increasing content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) broader adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together,
these drivers should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.
Importantly, WinWay's growth is increasingly being driven by higher socket content per

device rather than semiconductor unit growth alone and the stock currently trades at
16x of our 2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x, potential to re-rate with GM recovery
in the coming quarters. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Rising yields and utilisation to offset probe card dilution
Gross margin declined to 38.3% in Q226 from 45.3% in FY25, pressured by outsourced
MEMS probes and expansion-related costs. While probe-card margins are unlikely to

return to H125 levels of 48.9% given the Technoprobe partnership structure, we expect
margin pressure to ease as utilization and yields improve, supporting GM recovery to
43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in 2028E.
CPU and TPU as fundamental growth drivers
Beyond its core GPU socket franchise,  WinWay is well positioned to benefit from the
expansion of AI-oriented CPU and TPU platforms. We expect the company to supply
sockets for AMD's Venice CPU, Nvidia's Vera CPU, and Google's TPU v8t and v9, and

also Vera CPU probe cards, providing an additional growth engine beyond GPUs.
WinWay is well positioned in this segment due to its historical collaboration with end
customers and its proximity to the local Taiwanese test supply chain. We view AI CPUs
and TPUs as an increasingly important contributor to test interface demand as compute
architectures diversify in the agentic AI era, driving WinWay's socket and probe card
sales growth of 132%/75%/69% and 94%/72%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, respectively.

We estimate CPU/TPU to contribute  around 30%/20% of WinWay's 2027E sales.
Valuation: Initiate with a Buy rating, with price target of NT$8,900
We forecast EPS of NT$101.8 in 2026E, rising to NT$201.4/393.0 in 2027E/28E. Our NT
$8,900 price target is based on 30x average 2027E-28E P/E, in line with the company's
historical average. We believe this valuation is supported by WinWay's exposure to
structural growth in AI/HPC testing, expanding socket content, and our long-term

earnings CAGR estimate of 30%. We initiate with a Buy rating.

Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will capacity constraints limit WinWay's growth in the next two to three years?
Near term, yes. Beyond 2026E, no. WinWay's current capacity remains constrained, but ongoing
expansions should significantly increase output over the next two years. With new capacity coming

online and the company shifting toward higher-value AI/HPC applications, including CPU probe
cards, >10k-pin coaxial sockets and next-generation HyperSockets, we forecast revenue growth of
105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E, supported by capacity additions, rising AI/HPC content and
expanding socket demand. We also expect operating leverage to improve as capacity scales and
utilization increases, with OPM rising from 27.5% in 2026E to 32.1%/36.7% in 2027E/28E.

Q: Will WinWay's GM recover from Q226's 38.3%?
Yes. We view recent margin pressure as largely temporary. GM has been impacted by MEMS probe
sourcing through Technoprobe and start-up costs associated with capacity expansion. Although
margin dilution from probe card business is unlikely to unravel due to the MEMS partnership terms
with Technoprobe, as new facilities ramp and yields improve, we expect profitability to recover
gradually from the Q226 trough, with gross margin improving to 43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in

2028E.
We initiate coverage of WinWay with a Buy rating and NT$8,900 price target. We believe the market
is overly focused on near-term margin pressure and underappreciating WinWay's long-term growth
outlook. Key drivers include 1) expanding content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) growing adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together, these drivers
should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.

TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm  in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand as the majority are WinWay's customers. AI accelerator test times

have increased meaningfully for new platforms. We expect continued front-end and advanced
packaging investments to drive higher testing intensity and support long-term demand for advanced
sockets and test interfaces.
WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak and currently trades at 16x of our
2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market
price implies only a 19% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 25%, alongside long-term GM

level of 32% vs our estimate of 45%. We see scope for re-rating.

WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a semiconductor testing interface solutions
provider, with final test socket as  its main business segment. The company has high-end solutions for
AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test and burn-in test sockets and probe cards through
its own made probes and partnership with Technoprobe.

Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 1S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.

We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

greater sales CAGR comparing to the industry.
As the leader of AI/HPC sockets, WinWay is set to deliver 2026-31E socket sales CAGR
of 33%, underpinned by robust demand for final test and system-level test, with test

content and test times rising which each iteration of new AI accelerators/CPU, more
sockets and equipment will be needed in order to increase the throughput.

can also be inferred from AI accelerator/CPU roadmaps of Nvidia, AMD, Google,

WinWay is expanding capacity rapidly to capture this growing TAM. Industry test
interface suppliers are expanding capacity aggressively to meet rising AI and HPC
demand. As advanced semiconductors require higher-performance probe cards, sockets
and interface boards, capacity additions are becoming increasingly important to support

growth across both wafer-level and package-level testing.
We believe WinWay is well positioned to gain share within the high-end AI/HPC test
interface market. Taiwanese suppliers benefit from their proximity to leading fabless
customers and the growing preference for localised supply chains, particularly for
advanced testing applications where responsiveness and engineering support are
critical. Against a backdrop of tight industry supply, WinWay announced significant

capacity expansion plans over the next two to three years.
WinWay's capacity is expected by the company to increase from 3.5mn pins per month
at year-end 2025 to 6mn currently, reaching 9mn by year-end 2026 and 14mn by year-
end 2027 through expansions in Renwu, Kaohsiung. By 2027, approximately 60% of
pogo-pin requirements should be internally sourced, strengthening both supply security
and margins.
We estimate these capacity additions will support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71%

in 2026E/27E/28E.

For WinWay, this growth opportunity is substantial, as the capacity expansions for its
socket pins will satisfy around 60% of its own pogo pin  demand in 2027E, we estimate
socket sales will likely contribute for around NT$163 of EPS in 2027E and to further
expand to NT$307 in 2028E. This paves the way for substantial share gaining
opportunity for WinWay for AI/HPC sockets as we believe there are advantages to have

footprint within Taiwan, where there's already an established testing ecosystem with
testing providers such as ASE/SPIL/KYEC, and handler providers such as Hon Precision, all
positioned locally in Taiwan to provide around the clock service and support for AI/HPC
customers.
We see industry capacity tightness to support socket pin ASP growth, as we forecast pin
ASP to grow from US$2.0 in 2026E to US$2.5 in 2027E and US$3.0 in 2028E. We
believe WinWay's capacity expansion creates a significant growth opportunity with a

better margin profile. By 2027E, approximately 60% of pogo pin demand should be
internally sourced, improving both supply security and profitability. We estimate sockets
could contribute around NT$123 of EPS in 2027E, rising to NT$261 in 2028E. Combined
with its expanding capacity, strong customer relationships and proximity to Taiwan's
semiconductor testing ecosystem, including ASE/SPIL, KYEC and major handler
suppliers, WinWay is well positioned to gain share in the AI/HPC socket market.

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 6
The AI era has materially increased the value of semiconductor testing. Compared with
traditional mobile and automotive chips, AI accelerators incorporate larger compute
dies, more advanced nodes, chiplet architectures, advanced packaging technologies,
multiple I/O dies, and increasing HBM content. These factors drive higher-value test
consumables, including advanced MEMS probe cards, multilayer interface boards, and

next-generation sockets designed to support higher pin counts, thermal loading, and
power delivery requirements.
Capacity expansion by test interface vendors is ultimately supported by continued
investment throughout the advanced packaging ecosystem, from providers such as
TSMC, ASE, Intel, and Amkor. During the Q226 earnings calls, TSMC raised its recent
2026 capex from US$52-56bn to US$60-64bn and ASE raised its 2026 capex from US
$8.5bn to US$10.5bn, with both companies citing robust demand supporting capex

raise, we believe capex for the next two years will be even higher than 2026, indicating
continuous growth in future years. With advanced packaging technologies getting
significantly more complex and with larger form factor, illustrated by the upcoming
ramp of both TSMC's CoPoS (chip-on-panel-on-substrate) and Intel's EMIB-T
(embedded multi-die interconnect bridge) packaging technologies, test interface
solution providers will benefit with more value added consumables (see our CoPoS vs

EMIB-T deep dive).

New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC applications, are set to increase
in both front-end wafer fabrication advancements and back-end packaging
complexities with each iterations (i.e. Nvidia's roadmap from Blackwell to Rubin to

Feynman or Google's TPU roadmap from v7 to v8 to v9).   This trend is evident with
TSMC's front-end  and TSMC, Intel, and OSAT's back-end advanced packaging capacity
expansions. For front-end, we see industry N3 capacity expanding from 156kwpm in
year end 2025 to 224/283/293kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E, and N2 capacity from
47kwpm in year-end 2025 to 111/200/312kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E. For back-
end, we are seeing industry CoWoS capacity to rise from 90kwpm in 2025 to 160kwpm

in 2026E and further expand to 270kwpm in 2027E.

Final-test, system-level test, and burn-in content should continue to increase as AI

accelerators become larger, more power-hungry and more complex. Rising thermal
design power and packaging complexity increase validation requirements, driving

### Page 7

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 7
longer test times and higher demand for test equipment to prevent testing from
becoming a production bottleneck. This is where WinWay's sockets comes into play—
as high-end chips with 2.5D/3D/3.5D packaging requires intricate FT sockets to handle
the high thermal design power (TDP) and high data rates of up to 224G during the FT
process for reliable test results.

While architectural changes can affect test requirements for individual product
generations, we expect the long-term trend of rising testing content to remain intact.
For example, Nvidia's Rubin Ultra adopts a simpler package structure than previously
expected, from a 4-die structure to a 2-die structure and Rubin Ultra HBM de-spec from
HBM4E 12-Hi to initially use HBM4 8-Hi and upgrade to HBM4E 8-Hi at the latter stages,

which may moderate near-term test growth. However, future products such as Nvidia's
Feynman are expected to reintroduce greater package complexity, with a four-GPU per
package structure. More importantly, any reduction in test time can be offset by higher
production volumes, making throughput requirements equally important for testing
demand.
We expect test interface content growth to outpace unit growth, For instance, test
socket's ASP directly correlates with its pin counts, with the addition of 20-30% more

pins inside the sockets, socket ASP can grow 20-30% or more as the socket might
require significant cooling capabilities as the TDP of the DUT rises with its larger form
factor. Unlike prior semiconductor cycles, much of the current demand is driven by rising
test interface content per device rather than unit growth alone.
Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe WinWay is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers

and capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see three main growth drivers
for WinWay: 1) AI accelerators (GPU + TPU), 2) agentic AI driven CPU demand, and 3)
shift-left testing trend. Below we list the three main growth drivers in detail.
Accelerators still in focus with TPU as another significant growth driver
Nvidia has been the primary driver of testing content growth over the past several years,
benefiting the entire semiconductor testing supply chain. We expect this trend to

continue as GPU architectures evolve from Blackwell to Rubin and then to Feynman,
with increasing process node complexity, larger package sizes and more advanced
packaging technologies driving higher testing value per device.
We believe Nvidia should remain a major contributor to  WinWay, with GPUs continuing
to serve as their core growth engine. We estimate WinWay's socket share could improve
from a roughly 50/50 split with Smiths Interconnect in prior generations to 60/40 with

Rubin. Nvidia currently accounts for approximately 50% of WinWay's revenue. Both
Nvidia GPUs and Google TPUs increase testing demand through higher content per
device rather than unit growth alone.

### Page 8

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 8
Beyond Nvidia, Google's TPU roadmap represents a significant incremental growth
opportunity.  In our view, WinWay should benefit as the socket supplier for both TPU v8t
and the next-generation TPU v9 platform. We believe MediaTek's increasing share gain
for Google TPUs will also benefit the local Taiwanese test interface supply chain. We
estimate TPU to contribute to around 20% of WinWay 2027E sales.
We view TPU v9 as a particularly attractive opportunity given its large four-die package

architecture based on Intel's EMIB-T packaging technology, which should drive higher
testing content. We estimate production of 4mn TPU v8t units in 2027E, followed by
2.3mn TPU v9 packages (9.2mn dies) and an additional 2mn TPU v8t units in 2028E.
Importantly, while EMIB-T packaging is expected to be performed in Malaysia, we
believe the majority of advanced testing value should remain within the Taiwanese
testing ecosystem. Leading testing providers, including ASE and KYEC, maintain

substantial testing operations across Taiwan and Southeast Asia, supporting continued
participation by Taiwanese test interface suppliers. See our latest MediaTek report and
EMIB-T deep dive.
CPU business as a supplemental catalyst for test interface companies in
the agentic world
We believe semiconductor test interface are an underappreciated beneficiary of the
emerging agentic AI ecosystem. UBS estimates the CPU market will expand to US
$170bn by 2030E from US$31bn in 2025 and US$71bn in 2027E, driven by rising

deployment of AI infrastructure. As CPU-to-GPU ratios increasingly approach 1:1 in AI
clusters, demand for testing interfaces should expand alongside CPU volumes. TSMC is
the main CP test provider in the industry, with probe card suppliers aiding its massive
demand by supplying probes, such as TSMC's GPU probe cards supported by
Technoprobe's probes. However, with capacity now becoming scarce with strong front
and back-end demand, TSMC is outsourcing its CP business to ASE and KYEC, further

broadening the testing supply chain. This is where new probe card vendors come in—
smaller probe card players in the past, such as  WinWay, now has the opportunity to gain
significant, non-GPU share from TSMC, as they have MEMS probe capabilities
(WinWay's MEMS probe are from its strategic partnership with Technoprobe) and has a
long established relationship with the fabless companies such as Nvidia via supplying
non-probe card consumables such as sockets. Of which, CPU projects has become the

main area for  WinWay to expand their footprint.
We expect WinWay to benefit from the ramp of Nvidia's ARM-based Vera CPU and
Google's ARM-based Axion CPU in 2026E-27E. WinWay is positioned to supply sockets
and probe cards for Vera and sockets for Axion. Additional upside could come from
AMD's Venice CPU, where WinWay is well positioned. For WinWay, CPU-related
demand could account for approximately 30% of sales in 2026E-27E. Importantly, CPU
programmes diversify exposure beyond AI GPUs and provide a second structural growth

driver for test interface suppliers.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging. The
test shift-left trend will  drive structural AI/HPC probe card and test consumables

demand, with increasing test interface content as test insertions and test time increase.
WinWay should also benefit through its partnership with Technoprobe, with probe card
revenue growth remaining robust at 94%/72% in 2026E/27E following 161%/260%
growth in 2024/25.

The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while

defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed
testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million

(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall
integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of

wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge  another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level
chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

WinWay set to benefit from the AI trend with its leading

WinWay  was founded in 2001 in Kaohsiung, Taiwan, and is a leading provider of
semiconductor testing interface solutions, with test sockets representing its core
business. The company focuses on advanced AI/HPC applications, supplying sockets for

final test (FT), system-level test (SLT) and burn-in, as well as cobra probe cards  and
partnership with Technoprobe for MEMS probe cards.
WinWay's business is primarily divided into two segments:
 Test socket: Sockets are WinWay's largest revenue contributor. The company
supplies a broad range of testing sockets, including coaxial, PoP/plastic and burn-
in sockets, serving applications ranging from consumer electronics to high-
performance computing.

 Coaxial socket: utilised as the mainstream option for nearly all AI/HPC chips,
as traditional sockets are near its physical limits. Coaxial sockets need to handle
data rates of up to 224G for high speed transmission and high chip TDP during
the FT process.
 HyperSocket: a rubber-silicone like layer called elastomer are placed on top
of the pogo pins, these elastomer pins causes zero damage to the chips being
tested under high contact forces. WinWay's next generation HyperSocket

technology utilises elastomers to provide a softer contact between the DUT
and the pogo pins, avoiding damage to solder ball. This structure significantly
enhances spring probe's lifetime, reduces cleaning frequency, and achieves
maximum production efficiency. We estimate it will contribute around 5% of
sales in 2027E with a 20-30% ASP premium as compared to standard coaxial
socket.
 Probe cards:  WinWay historically supplies cobra probe cards (vertical probe

cards), but with the company announcing their five-year probe supply deal with
Technoprobe for MEMS probe in December 2025, they are now one of Nvida Vera
CPU's main probe card suppliers.
AI accelerators are pushing socket requirements beyond 10,000 pins, making coaxial
sockets the industry standard for leading-edge AI/HPC devices. These sockets must
support transmission speeds of up to 224G while handling significantly higher thermal
design power (TDP) during testing.

Elastomer technology is increasingly being adopted alongside spring-probe (pogo-pin)
architectures to reduce chip damage and improve contact reliability. WinWay's next-
generation HyperSocket integrates elastomer materials between the DUT and spring
probes, improving probe lifetime, reducing cleaning requirements and enhancing
production efficiency. We believe HyperSocket could become an important
differentiator as next-generation AI accelerators demand higher pin counts, bandwidth

and thermal performance. We estimate HyperSocket could contribute approximately
5% of WinWay's revenue in 2027E while carrying a 20-30% ASP premium versus
conventional coaxial sockets. The transition toward larger AI packages, higher pin
counts and faster data-transfer speeds should continue to increase socket content per
device. We expect this trend to support long-term growth in the socket market, with
technology leaders such as WinWay well positioned to gain share through next-

generation solutions including HyperSocket.
Beyond its existing product portfolio, WinWay is developing next-generation
technologies including CPO electrical test sockets and liquid-cooled sockets for future
high TDP AI accelerators, reinforcing its position within the AI/HPC testing ecosystem.

WinWay's competitive advantages are its 1) technology leadership with coaxial socket
and HyperSocket, 2) proximity to the semiconductor testing value chain in Taiwan, and
3) historical relationship with key AI companies such as Nvidia and AMD. We believe

these advantages may allow WinWay to capture significant upside in the growing
semiconductor testing TAM driven by cloud AI proliferation, and gain share across its
competition such as Smiths Interconnect for AI and HPC applications.

WinWay's margin is set to recover in 2027E/28E
Company GM hit a trough in Q226, at 38.3%. We view recent margin pressure as
largely temporary. GM has been impacted by MEMS probe sourcing through
Technoprobe (probe card GM now below 40% and dilutive to >50% coaxial socket GM)
and start-up costs associated with WinWay's rapid capacity expansion. During the early
part of 2025, WinWay's GM was high at 48.9% in H125, with more cobra probe cards

(60% GM) in mix with solid pogo pin and socket yield. With MEMS probe card now
accounting for more than 70% of probe card revenue and GM at around low to mid
30%, margin dilution from probe card business is unlikely to unravel for GM to return to
above 45% in the near term due to the MEMS partnership terms with Technoprobe.
However, we expect that as new facilities ramp and yields improve, profitability to
recover gradually from the Q226 trough. We see GM improving to 43.1% in 2027E and

44.7% in 2028E.

Appendix: What are semiconductor test sockets?
Semiconductor test socket are electro-mechanical interface that are used post chip
probing and chip packaging process to test the functionality of the chips. Sockets are
placed onto load boards on top of testers during the final test/system level test
processes. Handlers will place the chips onto the sockets, and pins inside the sockets will
enable the testers to read the signals from the device under test (DUT, i.e. the packaged

chips), with tester signals fan out  from beneath the PCB load board.
Figure 27: Socket illustration (with load
board underneath)
Source: UBS
As AI accelerators become larger and more complex, socket requirements continue to
increase. High-end sockets typically comprise a socket housing, alignment plate,
integrated thermal-control components and contact elements that connect the DUT to
the tester. Spring probes (pogo pins) are the most common choice for HPC sockets, with

high durability, individual probe replacement flexibility, and consistent contact force. FT
and SLT sockets account for more than 70% of the total socket market, with burn-in
sockets representing the remainder.

Despite a 120% YTD share-price increase, WinWay remains 43% below its April 2026
peak and trades at only 16x our 2028E P/E. We believe the market remains overly
focused on near-term GM pressure while underestimating the company's multi-year
growth opportunity. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 19% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 25%, alongside long-term
GM level of 32% vs our estimate of 45%.

We believe investors are not fully reflecting three key drivers:
1) CPU and TPU opportunities are becoming increasingly meaningful.
Beyond its core GPU socket franchise, WinWay is positioned to benefit from Nvidia's
Vera CPU, AMD's Venice CPU, and Google's TPU. These programmes should broaden
revenue sources and create an additional demand driver for high-end sockets and probe
cards.
2) Larger AI packages are driving higher socket content per device.

As AI accelerators adopt larger form factors, higher pin counts and greater power
consumption, socket complexity and ASPs continue to rise. We expect socket content
growth to outpace semiconductor unit growth, creating a structural tailwind for
WinWay.
3) Capacity ramp and margin recovery are not fully reflected.
Following substantial capacity expansion, we expect revenue growth of
105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E. At the same time, we forecast gross margin

recovery from 38.3% in Q226 to 43.1%/44.7% in 2027E/28E as utilization and yields
improve.
Our NT$8,900 price target is based on our valuation for WinWay at 30x average 2027E-
28E P/E, in-line with its historical average of 30x and underpinned by a 30% 2027-31E
earnings CAGR. Comparing with historical NTM peer average PE of roughly 25x
(currently trading at 39x NTM PE), we believe WinWay deserves to trade at a premium
given its leadership in AI/HPC sockets, expanding CPU and TPU exposure, and our long-

term earnings CAGR estimate.
WinWay is trading at NT$6,130 as of
6 Oct 2026

Company Description
Company Description
WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a
semiconductor testing interface solution provider, with final test
sockets as its main business segment. The company has high-end
solutions for AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test
and burn-in test sockets and probe cards through its own-made
probes and a partnership with Technoprobe.

Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC
applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Forecast excess return 39.9%
Company Description
WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a semiconductor testing interface
solution provider, with final test sockets as its main business segment. The company has high-
end solutions for AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test and burn-in test sockets

and probe cards through its own-made probes and a partnership with Technoprobe.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value WinWay based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less

investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in WinWay
gaining/losing socket/probe card orders from major end customers, foundries, and OSATs.

- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
WinWay losing market share from major end customers, foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
WinWay, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.

- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future socket/probe card orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact WinWay’s
margins for socket/probe card shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

주가 추이

3Q 큰 폭의 판매 감소, 매출/영업이익 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 각각 44.6조원(-4.5% 이하 Y oY)과 2.4조원
(-5.4%) 기록하며 시장 기대치 하회 전망. 3Q 매출액은 글로벌 판매 부진 지속
과 환율 하락, 그리고 친환경차 판매 성장 둔화 등의 영향에 기인. 특히, 3Q 판
매량은 918.7만대(-11.5% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동기 대비 큰 폭 감소하며 매출
액에 부정적 영향. 이는 7월과 8월 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생
산 물량 차질이 발 생 한 부분에 기인. 파업은 기존 물량/믹스 개선에 기 여 했 던 
글로벌 HEV 판매도 둔화(+3.4%)시키면서 매출에 부정적 영향을 심화. 
3Q 수 익 성 은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입에 따른 원가 상승 영향과 친환경차 
판매 감소에 따른 부정적 믹스 영향 불 가 피. 이에 시장 기대치인 3.0조원을 크
게 하회할 전망. 다만, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지면서 판매보증비 
외화환산손익 영향은 다소 개선(0.4%p) 예상되며, 관세 영향 또한 전년 동기 
대비 약 1조원(2.1%p) 감소하면서 수익성 악화 영향을 일부 만 회 할 전망.  
’27년 지연된 신차효과 가시화로 반등 여력 충분 
4Q부터는 3Q 파업 영향 등으로 지연된 신차 확대 본격화되고, 현지 수요 높은 
차종 중심의 물량 확대를 통해 매출 회복 및 이익 성장 가시화가 기대. 아 반 떼
와 투싼 FMC 확대로 국내 및 해외 판매 둔화가 점진적 만회되고, 지난 9월부
터 터키에서 아이오닉3 생산이 본격화되면서 유럽에서의 시장 부진 회복이 기
대. 또한, GV80과 G80 HEV 모델 출시로 내년부터 국내/북미 중심 판매 확대
되며 올해 부진했던 물량/믹스 개선에 기여할 것으로 기대. ’27년 HMGMA에
서 HEV 신차 생산 투입 예정되어 있어 상 대 적 으 로 높은 수요가 지속되는 북미
에서의 HEV 현지 생산 확대로 가동률 증 가 와 관세 부담 완화도 수익성 개선에 
기여할 것으로 기대. 
부정적 환율 고려 필요하나 ’27년 강한 증익 기대  
’27년 동사 영 업 이 익 추 정 치 는 12.4조원(+22.3%)으로 ’26년 감 익 에 서 ’27년 
증익 전환 예상. 최근 환율 추세 고려 시 내년 상 반 기 까 지 부정적인 환율 영향 
확대 가 능 성 존재하나, 기존 노후 차 종 에 서 현지 수요 대응 가능한 신차 중심 
생산/판매 확대로 물량/믹스 개선되며 부 정 적 환율 영향 상쇄 기대.  
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-11.8%)과 상반기 적 용 한 코 스 피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)에 따라 목 표 주 가 를 670,000원으로 하향. 그러나, 연 내 RMAC 운영 
성 과 와 RA 설립, 12 월 모셔널 자 율 주 행 상용화와 웨 이 모向 로 보 택 시 
양산/공급 등 미래 사 업 에 서 의 가 시 성 도 여전히 기대 가능한 부분.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

HiMSEN 엔진 및 SMR 주기기 생산설비 대규모 증설 발표 
- 9/10 HD현대중공업은 총 1조 722억원 규모의 시설투자 2건을 공시 
① 대출력 발전엔진(4행정 중속엔진, HiMSEN엔진) 생산능력 확충에 8,336
억원 투입, 글로벌 전력수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 확대에 대응하는 목
적. 대출력 발전엔진 전용 공장 신설과 토지 취득, 건축물·기계설비 등 생산설
비 구축이 골자. 투자기간은 ‘26.09~’28.05(설계/인허가 ~’27.02, 공장건설 
~’28.05. ’28.06 상업운전개시, 28년 가동률 30%, 2030년 100% 도달) 
② SMR 주기기 제작 생산시설 구축에 2,386억원(자기자본 대비 2.55%) 투
입, SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주 물량 증가에 대응. SMR 전용 공장 
및 설비 신설과 기존 공장 최적화를 병행, 투자기간은 ’26.10~’29.04. 
기다리던 증설 공시. 늘어난 Capa로 AI DC향 수혜 확대 기대 
- 25년 3.0GW(선박용 2.3GW + 육상용 0.7GW)였던 HiMSEN 엔진 생산 
Capa는 2030년 7.2GW(선박용 3.2GW + 육상용 4.0GW)로 확대. 그간 선
박용 중심이었던 엔진 사업의 포트폴리오를 발전용으로 본격 다각화하는 사
업 재편으로 해석 
- AI DC 신규 증설 확대 추세 속 계통 연결의 어려움과 CCGT, Aero -
derivative 대비 납기 및 부하상승시간, 모듈화 용이성 영역에서 장점을 가진 
발전용 중속엔진에 대한 수요 지속 기대(260908 당사 보고서 참조) 
- 지난 Corban Energy Group 향 계약 건(1,000MW, $ 674 mn)을 단순 
적용 시 금번 증설의 향후 매출액 기여규모는 연간 $ 2.8 bn에 달함. 엔진 시
장의 Seller-Market 기조에 따라 20%를 상회하는 높은 이익률이 지속될 것
으로 전망함에 따라 향후 유의미한 이익 성장 기대 
- HD현대마린솔루션 또한 LTSA 수주 저변 확대 측면에서 향후 이익 확장 가
능. HiMSEN엔진 고유 라이선스 보유에 따른 시너지로 그룹사 전반의 수혜

Document 158: AI泡沫担忧升温，台积电送来“定心丸”：未来三年资本开支将远超千亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI泡沫担忧升温，台积电送来“定心丸”：未来三年资本开支将远超千亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

台积电管理层确认 2026 年资本⽀出 600 ⾄ 640 亿美元，并明确 2026 ⾄ 2028 年累计⽀出将⼤
幅超越过去三年约 1010 亿美元总量。摩根⼤通认为这为上游半导体设备商提供了跨财年的
⻓期订单保障。其中业务⾼度依赖台积电等代⼯客⼾的设备商，将最直接地享受这⼀波资
本开⽀红利。
摘要：
台积电公布强劲三季度业绩，并释放出远超市场预期的未来投资信号。
台积电于 10 ⽉ 15 ⽇公布三季度财报，单季营收达 466 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 16% ，较彭博市场⼀致预
期⾼出约 2% 。公司四季度营收指引中值为 533 亿美元，超出⼀致预期约 6% ，全年增速指引达
44% 。
更受市场关注的是，管理层确认 2026 年资本⽀出维持 600 亿⾄ 640 亿美元区间不变，并进⼀步明
确表⽰ 2026 ⾄ 2028 年三年合计资本⽀出将⼤幅超越 2023 ⾄ 2025 年约 1010 亿美元的历史总量。
追⻛交易台消息， 10 ⽉ 16 ⽇，摩根⼤通 Mio Shikanai 团队发表报告，认为公司管理层不仅维持

了近期投资指引，更将资本⽀出的确定性延伸⾄ 2028 年乃⾄ 2030 年前后，为半导体设备商提供
了超越单⼀财年的⻓期订单能⻅度。
摩根⼤通指出，在晶圆代⼯客⼾营收占⽐较⾼的设备商将从中受益最为直接。在设备需求旺
盛、供应偏紧的背景下，台积电提前与供应商共享投资计划的做法，有助于缓解短期供应链压
⼒，同时也进⼀步巩固了头部设备商的战略地位。
扩产路线清晰，建⼚计划覆盖中国台湾及多个海外市场
台积电管理层披露了详细的产能扩张时间表。在中国台湾，公司计划建设 13 座先进芯⽚及先进
封装晶圆⼚；在海外，则规划新增五⾄六座晶圆⼚。
按制程节点来看，A14 技术预计 2027 年进⼊⻛险试产， 2028 年实现量产。 N2 制程⽅⾯，公司计
划于 2027 年将产能翻倍。 N3 制程的扩产⼒度超出此前预期。
台南原有新⼚计划于 2027 年上半年投产，但因客⼾需求强劲，管理层决定额外新建⼀座台南⼯
⼚，预计 2028 年上半年开始量产。
海外⽅⾯，美国亚利桑那州第⼆座晶圆⼚建设已完⼯，⽬前正处于设备安装阶段，管理层计划

于 2027 年三季度开始量产，进度略超原定计划。
⽇本熊本第⼆座晶圆⼚（ JASM ）的 N3 制程量产则预计于 2028 年启动。公司整体亦持续推进由
N5 向 N3 的全⾯切换。
资本⽀出超越历史区间， 2030 年前后或再度跃升
尽管 2026 年资本开⽀区间维持 600 亿⾄ 640 亿美元不变，管理层就中⻓期⽀出⾛势释放了更为积
极的信号。
台积电明确表⽰， 2026 ⾄ 2028 年累计资本开⽀将⼤幅⾼于过去三年约 1010 亿美元的总量，显⽰
出更为陡峭的投资加速曲线。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

管理层还前瞻性地提及，公司计划于 2030 年前后开始引⼊⾼数值孔径极紫外（ High-NA EUV ）
光刻设备，届时资本⽀出⽔平可能显著⾼于当前。
⽬前设备、材料及劳动⼒成本均呈快速上涨态势，但管理层表⽰，随着投资稳步推进，营收增
速应能持续超越资本⽀出增速。

Key points
• The Cotality home value index –
covering all of Australia – fell 1.1% in
September following a downwardly
revised declines of 1.2% in August

and 1.4% in July. Annual growth
stalled flat with prices nationally now
5.2% below their peak in March.
• Declines continue to be widespread.
Across the major cities, prices fell
1.4% in Sydney, 1.5% in Brisbane,
1.3% in Adelaide, and 1.2% in Perth
with Melbourne recording a slightly
milder 0.7% decline. Sydney and
Melbourne continue to see more
pronounced declines with prices now
down 7%yr and 6.2%yr respectively.
• Preliminary estimates show the number

of sales, in rolling 3mth terms, down
3.9%qtr and 17.4%yr. The pace of
turnover falls has moderated from
the rapid 8.6%qtr recorded in July.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia,
fell 1.1% in September with downward revisions to the
previous two months again shaving a further 0.4% off prices.
The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2% from their

March peak. Annual growth is flat.
The price correction remains broad, Cotality reporting 97% of
capital city suburbs experienced falls over the three months
to September, Darwin the only city to record gains. Sydney
and Melbourne continue to see more pronounced declines,
albeit moderating a touch, particularly in Melbourne.
Against this, declines in Brisbane, Adelaide and Perth have
accelerated over the last few months. Across segments, unit
prices and lower price tiers continue to track slightly milder

falls than houses and top tiers.
Seasonal variations accentuated price weakness through winter
but are now neutral. The peak-to-trough price decline nationally
is slightly milder at 4.6% in seasonally adjusted terms. Unless
specified, figures in titles, tables and the commentary below are
in non-seasonally adjusted terms, consistent with the ‘headline’
figures reported by Cotality. However, figures shown in charts
are in seasonally adjusted terms.

Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 3.9%qtr in September and 17.4%yr with previous
estimates revised 1-2ppts lower. The monthly pattern
suggests small gains in July and August have given way to
another sizeable fall in September.
Looking at the price performance across different jurisdictions:
• Sydney prices dropped 1.4% fall in September, monthly
declines averaging 1.6% over the previous four months,
tracking a 17% annualised pace of decline since May. All

segments and tiers recorded falls in Q3 but with top and
middle tier houses notably weaker.
• Melbourne prices fell 0.7%mth, a slower pace than other
capitals. This likely reflects the weaker starting point coming
into the correction, prices already back to the levels seen in
mid-2021. As elsewhere, 'top tier' houses prices are leading
the decline. 'Low tier' unit prices mostly held their ground in
Q3, prices in this sub-segment already plumbing 10yr lows.

• Brisbane was the weakest capital city price-wise, recording
a 1.5% drop in September with previous monthly declines
also revised down heavily (August from 1% to 1.6% and
July from 1.2% to 1.7%). The combined effect has taken
the rolling 3mth change to –4.7%qtr from –2.7%qtr last
month and –0.6%qtr in July. At 5.9%yr, annual growth is
still positive but losing altitude quickly, having run at 18%yr
as recently as May. Sellers have been slow to respond to

3WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
the sudden stop with total listings up over 50% vs a year
ago, albeit still not high compared to long run averages
(reflecting the extremely tight starting point for the Brisbane
market).
• Adelaide dwelling prices fell 1.3%mth, the August decline

also marked down from 0.8% to 1.1%, annual price growth
dropping to 6.5%yr. The price correction began later in
Adelaide but is now running at a similar pace to prices
nationally.
• Perth recorded a 1.2% decline but also saw big downward
revisions to previous months (August from 0.8% to –1.7%
and July from –1.3% to –1.8%). At 10.1%yr, annual growth
is still solidly positive but was tracking at over 25%yr earlier
in the year. As with Adelaide and Brisbane, the detail shows

a more even spread of declines across price tiers. However,
turnover is showing a milder pull-back in Adelaide.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.4%mth, 11.9%yr), relatively
resilient in Hobart (–0.5%mth, 7%yr) but weaker in
Canberra (–1.1%mth, –1.6%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.3%). Annual
prices growth is in the 3.4-6.3%yr range.

Our recently released Housing Pulse report (here) provides
a deeper diver into the situation in Australia's housing
markets. While the absence of selling pressure, still tight
supply-demand balance and sentiment mix suggests the
correction was still relatively 'orderly' through the September
quarter, the latest monthly data shows momentum remains
unambiguously negative.
That said, there are clearly more headwinds to come. The
RBA latest interest rate rise and what now looks to be a

more likely prospect of a follow-up increase at its meeting in
early November will impact in coming months. Next week's
consumer sentiment update will show how the change, and
the latest round of weak updates, is shaping housing-related
sentiment. Either way, momentum looks set to remain weak
into year-end and, barring a significantly improved inflation
and interest rate outlook, early 2027.

rose

%

,

3

)
.

.

.
3

from

(vs
+
3
.
6

)
.

(+

.

1

13
.
1

.

.

,

3
%
.

.

Document 204: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

Document 256: How AI agents could reshape retail as stores move beyond sales

Source PDF: all report pdf/How AI agents could reshape retail as stores move beyond sales.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Agentic commerce, where autonomous AI agents
independently select and purchase products on behalf of
consumers, is forcing retailers to rethink their role. As AI takes
over more of the buying process, physical stores are evolving
from a sales channel into a destination for advice, service,
experience, and omnichannel support
Katinka Jongkind
A Galaxy General AI robot retail store, Shanghai. Retail is entering a new phase
of digital disruption, where agentic commerce could fundamentally change

how consumers shop
Agentic commerce fundamentally changes
the buying process
Retail is entering a new phase of digital disruption. E-commerce
has already transformed where consumers shop; agentic
commerce is set to change how they shop. Autonomous artiﬁcial
intelligence agents can search, compare and purchase products on
behalf of consumers, shifting shopping from an active task to an
increasingly automated background process.

### Page 2

Agentic commerce goes beyond AI
assistants and comparison websites
Key diﬀerences between agentic commerce, AI assistants (such as
ChatGPT and Gemini) and comparison websites
Source: ING Research
Most relevant for routine purchases, less so
for luxury goods
Agentic commerce is not equally suited to every product category.
Its greatest potential lies in repeat and replacement purchases, as
well as functional, standardised products. In these categories, AI

agents can eﬃciently compare prices, speciﬁcations, and
availability, while using consumption patterns to determine the
optimal time to buy.
Adoption is likely to be slower for luxury, trend-sensitive and
experience-driven products. In these categories, inspiration,
emotion, and personal preference remain central to the purchasing
decision, making consumers less inclined to relinquish control. AI
agents can assist by pre-selecting products that match predeﬁned

preferences, but ﬁnal purchasing decisions are likely to remain
ﬁrmly in the hands of the consumer.

### Page 3

Consumer electronics and personal care
lead the adoption of agentic commerce
Within non-food retail, agentic commerce appears particularly well
suited to consumer electronics and personal care. Both sectors are
characterised by a high degree of product standardisation and a
large share of repeat purchases, making them ideal candidates for
AI-driven purchasing.
In consumer electronics, purchasing decisions are often based on
objective and easily comparable criteria, including price, technical

speciﬁcations, and availability. This makes products such as
televisions, laptops and other electronic devices well suited to
automation, allowing AI agents to manage much of the purchasing
journey from product selection to the ﬁnal transaction.
The potential is equally signiﬁcant in personal care, where many
purchases are routine and repetitive. Products such as diapers,
shampoo, and toothpaste can be reordered automatically based
on usage patterns and preset preferences. By reducing the eﬀort

required to order everyday products, AI agents could further
accelerate the shift towards online shopping.
Agentic commerce is best suited to
consumer electronics and personal care
products
Product categories most and least suited to agentic commerce

### Page 4

Source: ING Research
Personal taste limits AI agents' potential in
fashion and living
For now, fashion and home furnishings appear less suited to fully
autonomous agentic commerce. Purchasing decisions in these
categories are often shaped by inspiration, aesthetics, personal
taste, and broader lifestyle considerations, making consumers less
willing to hand over control to an AI agent.
While AI can support product discovery and narrow down options

based on predeﬁned preferences, the ﬁnal purchase decision will
typically remain with the consumer. In these segments, AI is
therefore more likely to serve as a decision-support tool than as a
fully autonomous purchasing agent.
Chinese platforms lead adoption
Agentic commerce creates the greatest opportunities for large
global marketplaces and technology companies. These players
combine vast product assortments with the scale, data and
infrastructure needed to deploy AI agents eﬀectively. Their

integrated ecosystems, including payment solutions, logistics
networks, and data platforms, enable them to integrate AI agents
into their business operations faster and more eﬀectively.
Chinese platforms such as Alibaba and JD.com currently lead the
way, with AI agents already deeply integrated into their respective
platform networks. These agents can not only provide product
Cookie settings

### Page 5

recommendations but also complete purchases autonomously.
Meanwhile, US technology companies including Amazon, Google,
Meta, OpenAI and Anthropic are investing heavily in the
development and rollout of AI agents, intensifying the global race
for leadership in this emerging market.
European players are structurally lagging behind their American
and Chinese competitors. Stricter regulation and less integrated
platform networks limit their ability to bundle scale, data and

services. While Zalando is among Europe’s frontrunners in AI-
powered retail, its applications are currently focused on product
discovery, comparison, and selection. Fully autonomous
purchasing has yet to become a reality.
Technology platforms are becoming the
gatekeepers of commerce
AI agents are rarely neutral actors. Embedded within commercial
ecosystems, they are inherently linked to the interests of platforms,
marketplaces and technology companies. Although agentic

commerce promises greater convenience and time savings for
consumers, it is also likely to strengthen the dominance of large
tech ﬁrms and marketplaces. These parties are becoming
increasingly inﬂuential in shaping product choices and controlling
consumer access.
Physical stores are not disappearing, but
their role is changing
How will the role of the physical store evolve in an AI-driven retail
landscape?
Cookie settings

### Page 6

Source: ING Research
Redeﬁning the store in the age of AI
Agentic commerce is expected to accelerate the shift of sales to
online channels, putting further pressure on the existence of
physical stores. The extent of this impact will largely depend on
consumers’ willingness to delegate purchasing decisions to AI
agents.
Physical stores will nevertheless remain relevant for many non-food
retailers. Their role is shifting from sales channel to destination for

advice, service, inspiration, and support of online sales. Stores that
continue to rely mainly on transactional sales risk losing relevance
in an increasingly AI-driven retail landscape.
Retailers that successfully integrate their physical and digital
channels, deﬁne clear roles for their stores, and deploy technology
strategically can strengthen their position within the customer
journey. In a future shaped by AI-driven commerce, their role will

increasingly centre on adding value where automation cannot.

Document 300: Rates Spark_ Don’t expect a break back below 5%

We think the US 10yr stays above 5%, and in fact heads back
up to 5.25%. That won't help the eurozone, which continues to
grapple with the problem that is France. Probably overdone, but
far from over
Padhraic Garvey, CFA
We think the 10-year yield on US Treasuries will remain above 5.00% and head
back up to 5.25%
The mood for US Treasuries has calmed.
But don't get too comfortable
Treasury market moves of late have been quite something. There
were buyers in the 5.3% area post the CPI release on Wednesday.

Then the Asian open coincided with some large falls in equities,
with a particular focus on Korean equities, which prompted some
follow-on buying of Treasuries. Note, however, that there have been
decent buyers of Treasuries on an ongoing basis in the past
number of weeks. It just has not translated into a meaningful move
lower in yields, mostly as it had been oﬀset by net shorts on
Treasury futures. We've been overdue a reversal.

### Page 2

That said, we're not convinced this is a structural move, and we'd
be quite surprised if we were to break below 5% on the 10yr yield.
Treasuries did not react much to Thursday's data, but market
participants will be aware that we had another good (low) jobless
claims number, retail sales were ﬁrm and PPI inﬂation
uncomfortably high. The market will likely use this as a platform
from which to rebuild higher in yields over the weeks ahead, and
into the mid-terms; especially with neither side voicing an intention

to take action on the ﬁscal deﬁcit. The issues of high printed
inﬂation and a high deﬁcit remain.
The balance of probabilities suggests there is at least one more leg
of long-end weakness still to play out. This could take the US 10-
year yield back into the 5.25% to 5.5% range. Other markets would
likely move in tandem, mapping out similar, albeit less dramatic,
blow-outs. With 5.25% looking like a mean-reversion level, we think
the US 10-year yield could overshoot towards 5.5% and remain

elevated for a number of months as bond market jitters persist.
This could quite easily extend into the ﬁrst quarter of 2027. Our
views here are unchanged, despite recent moves.
The eurozone remains in the throes of an
uneasy holding pattern at dodgy levels
The focus in the eurozone is increasingly insular, with France front
and centre and setting the mood. Here too, there has been some
market stability – but at uncomfortable levels. The standout move

has been a doubling of the French 10yr swap spread from the 60bp
area to the 120bp area. And French spreads to Germany are
practically as wide as they've ever been since the dawn of the euro,
even versus the immediate pre-EMU years. The safe narrative to
reach for is the debt dynamics one, and the lack of ambition to
correct it. Made worse, in fact, by the political options being oﬀered
for 2028.
This is probably overdone, as a swing to the right (should it occur)

could have some tail positives that aren't currently properly
discounted. Look at the improvement seen in Italian spreads
following the rise of Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni. It's been gradual

but persistent, and has not been overly impacted by French
concerns. In that sense, the Italian response has been low beta in

characterisation, which is good.
In fact, the ﬁrst real bout of widening in French spreads can be
traced back to President Emmanuel Macron's election gamble of
mid-2024. Things subsequently calmed, but were not forgotten.
The recent spike is an ampliﬁcation and extension of an underlying
tension that has not gone away. So, overdone? Probably, yes. But
we also remain in a danger zone for bonds generally. Just like in the
US, more overshoots to the upside are entirely foreseeable.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Retail sales growth is expected to 
accelerate from 4.5% yoy in July to 7.8% 
in August, supported by an increase in 
visitor arrivals. The daily average of 
mainland visitor arrivals rebounded from 
2.7% yoy in July to 13.3% in August. This 
significantly outpaced the growth in 
outbound tourism  to 10.7% in August. 
The overall diffusion index improved 
during the month, with both the retail 
trade and restaurant sectors showing 
gains. Consumer prices remained steady 
at 1.7% yoy in August, while the prices of 
durable goods and food improved to 0.8% 
and 0.6% yoy, respectively.

Indonesia

September inflation is expected to stay 
high but not accelerate sharply from 
August. Headline CPI inflation is 
projected to rise marginally to 3.3% yoy 
in September from 3.2% in August. 
Continued rise in food prices, notably 
rice and perishables, is expected to be 
offset by steady subsidised fuel costs. 
Official price stabilisation measures and 
inter-province supply adjustments will 
help to limit the spillover impact of El 
Niño risks. Evolving inflation dynamics 
are consisted with the BI’s decision to 
pause on rates, shifting attention instead 
towards managing near -term rupiah 
volatility and external risks.

South Korea

September trade and inflation data will 
be the key focus in the week ahead. 
Exports are expected to remain strong at 
around 60% yoy, supported by front -
loading of shipments ahead of the 
Chuseok holiday (78.3% growth in the 
first 20 days of September). Headline 
CPI is expected to remain around 3% yoy, 
driven by festive demand for food and 
services ahead of Chuseok. We maintain 
our forecast for the Bank of Korea to 
deliver one more 25bps rate hike to 3.25% 
in 4Q. We do not rule out an additional 
25bps hike to 3.50% if elevated global 
oil prices prove m ore persistent, adding 
further upside pressure to CPI in 4Q.

Singapore

We expect Singapore’s industrial 
production growth to surge to 19.0% yoy 
in August 2026, sharply higher than the 
6.8% yoy  in July. This was partly 
flattered by favourable base effects, as 
August 2025 marked the lowest monthly 
reading of that year. The city -state’s 
factory expansion continued to be driven 
primarily by strength in electronics and 
precision engineering, both of  which are 
benefitting from the global artificial 
intelligence (AI) infrastructure build -out 
and sustained demand for AI hardware.

Vietnam 
We expect Vietnam’s real GDP growth to 
accelerate to 9.4% yoy in 3Q26, stepping 
up from 8.4% yoy  in 2Q26. This growth 
surge was driven by broad -based 
strength across electronics production 
and exports, foreign and public

### Page 3

The Week Ahead September 25, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

investments, and retail spending, 
notwithstanding evolving geopolitical 
developments. The stronger momentum 
was corroborated by high -frequency 
indicators from July -August, while 
September’s data will likely be robust. In 
July-August, i ndustrial production 
expanded by an average of 14.9% yoy 
(from 11.2% yoy average in 2Q26), goods 
exports grew by 25.5% yoy (gaining from 
22.8% yoy in 2Q26), public investments 
remained robust, alongside  resilient 
domestic and tourism spending, even as 
inflation remained elevated. 
Consequently, we see upside risks to our 
already-bullish 2026 GDP growth 
forecast of 8.0%.

Document 348: US MARKETS CALL_ Is The 10-Year Bond Yield Back To The Old Normal_

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKETS CALL_ Is The 10-Year Bond Yield Back To The Old Normal_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-10
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKETS CALL: Is The 10-Year Bond Yield Back To The Old Normal?
Date:    15 hours ago
US MARKETS CALL: Is The 10-Year Bond Yield Back To The Old Normal?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKETS CALL: Is The 10-
Year Bond Yield Back To The
Old Normal?
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 10 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Justin Simmonds / Unsplash
I. Bonds
The Economist just rang the all-clear siren for the bond market (chart). It is
safe to buy bonds now that the front cover of the October 10 magazine is
titled "Will bonds blow up?" This and other national magazines’ cover
stories often have been great contrary indicators. It’s the “front-page

### Page 3

curse” (once magazines put in the time it takes them to produce a cover
article on a financial market trend, investors have moved on).
We think the 10-year Treasury bond yield is back to normal this year, i.e.,
in the 4.00% to 5.00% range (chart). We've disputed the widespread
notion that "interest rates are likely to remain higher for longer." That
implies that they should come back down at some point. We've argued
that "interest rates are likely to stay normal for longer." They were

abnormally low between the Great Financial Crisis and the Great Virus
Crisis, when central banks rigged the fixed-income markets with zero and
near-zero interest-rate policies and quantitative easing.
As bond yields rose in recent months to the top end of what we consider
the normal range, we expected buyers to be attracted by higher yields, so
we expected the range to be maintained (that’s what happened in in 2023,
when the yield spiked to 5.00% in late October). This time, the yield

### Page 4

spiked above the normal range, to around 5.25% (as it did during the
4.00%-5.00% normal-range period from 2002-07).
In recent conversations with several of our institutional accounts, many
expressed interest in buying bonds at levels above 5.00%.
A slice-and-dice analysis of the 10-year yield shows that its rise this year
has been almost entirely attributable to the rise in the comparable TIPS
yield (chart). The spread, which is a proxy for inflation expectations over

the next 10 years, has been range-bound roughly between 2.00% and
2.50% since 2023.
Since 2023, the TIPS yield has closely tracked the Weekly Economic
Index, which tracks the growth rate in real GDP on a y/y basis (chart). This

### Page 5

supports our view that the increase in US nominal yields has been driven
by better-than-expected economic growth.
In the past, the expected inflation spread was highly correlated with the
price of a barrel of crude oil (chart). That correlation has been weaker
since 2023. Bond investors seem to believe that inflation will remain low in
the 2.00%-2.50% y/y range over the rest of the Roaring 2020s into the
Roaring 2030s. We agree.
Nevertheless, we can't rule out the possibility that another spike in oil

prices, driven by a re-escalation of the war in the Middle East, won't push
nominal bond yields higher. If so, that will likely be another buying
opportunity.

### Page 6

By the way, the expected inflation spread closely tracks the 10-year
forward inflation-linked swap (chart).
All major forward inflation-linked swaps are roughly in the 2.00%- 2.50%
range (chart).
Meanwhile, the link between the 13-week change in the 10-year nominal
yield and the Citigroup Economic Surprise Index suggests that the
former's recent climb should abate (chart).

### Page 7

II. Stocks
The steep rise in bond yields since mid-August has weighed on the S&P
500 equal-weight stock price index, while the market-weight index rose to
record highs last week (chart). The former may be finding support at its
200-day moving average.
The gap between XMAGS's outperformance and MAGS's
underperformance has narrowed since mid-August as bond yields jumped
(chart). Over the past couple of weeks, they both did well.

### Page 8

The Russell 2000 also dipped as bond yields soared since the late
summer (chart). The index is back at its 200-day moving average, which
should hold if US bond yields continue to stabilize.
III. Sentiment
Investor sentiment turned more bullish last week according to the two bull-
bear ratios we track. (chart). Like the front-page curse, they also tend to
be contrary indicators. However, they aren't bullish enough to be bearish
for stocks.

### Page 9

clause.

Source PDF: all report pdf/「社外取締役が議長」は何を変えるのか ～プライム市場で118社、大型企業で選任進む～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026 年 10 月 8 日

1/4 
「社外取締役が議長」は何を変えるのか 
～プライム市場で 118 社、大型企業で選任進む～ 
 資産運用調査部 シニア研究員  河谷 善夫

1．社外取締役の取締役会議長選任は緩やかに拡大 
筆者は、昨年8 月中旬時点でのプライム市場上場会社のうち、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選
任している会社について分析したレポート（河谷、2025）を公表し、2022 年 7 月上旬時点より、
その数が増加していることを示した。本稿では、2026 年9 月末の時点でのプライム市場における
社外取締役の取締役会議長選任の状況をフォローアップした上で、最後に社外取締役の取締役会
議長選任の意味について考える。

資料 1 社外取締役の取締役会議長選任会社数の推移

（出所）東証公表資料を基に第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

資料 1 のとおり、2025 年 8 月から2026 年 9 月にかけて会社数は9 社増加し、プライム市場での
比率は 6.7％から7.6％と0.9％ポイント上昇した。2022 年以降、年平均で概ね1％ポイントの上
昇ペースが続いている。このグラフだけでは見えないが、2025 年8 月から2026 年9 月にかけて継
続してプライム市場に上場している会社では17 社が社外取締役を新たに取締役会議長に選任して
おり（注1）、新規選任の動きは続いている。 
また、118 名中 117 名が独立社外取締役であり、社外取締役議長はほぼすべて独立社外取締役で
ある（注2）。

2/4 
2．特定の機関設計に限らず普及、ただし社外取締役議長はなお少数 
資料 2 は、会社の機関設計別の2022 年、2025 年、2026 年のプライム市場での社外取締役の取
締役会議長選任会社数と割合を示したものである。

資料 2 機関設計別の社外取締役の取締役会議長選任状況（プライム市場）

（出所）資料 1 と同じ

2026 年では、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任している会社は、監査役設置会社で5.3％、監
査等委員会設置会社で6.0％、指名委員会等設置会社で42.7％である。指名委員会等設置会社の
割合は依然として高いが、2022 年からの増加状況をみると、社外取締役を取締役会議長とする動
きは、指名委員会等設置会社以外にも徐々に広まっており、昨年のレポートで確認した傾向は続
いているといえる。 
なお、これも資料で示していないが、取締役の過半を独立社外取締役とする会社でも、社外取
締役を取締役会議長とする会社は約2 割にとどまり、2025 年 8 月から2026 年 9 月にかけて大きな
変化はなかった（注3）。

3．社長を議長とする会社の減少と、大型企業で先行する社外取締役議長選任 
資料 3 では、2022 年、2025 年、2026 年のプライム市場での取締役会議長の属性の変化をグラフ
として示した。

資料 3 取締役会議長の属性の変化（プライム市場）

（注）「その他の取締役等」には、会長・社長以外の執行役を兼任する取締役を含む。 
（出所）資料 1 と同じ

### Page 3

3/4 
2022 年からの変化をみると、社長を取締役会議長とする会社の割合は71.4％から65.6％へ
5.8％ポイント低下する一方、社外取締役を議長とする会社の割合は、3.6％から 7.6％へ4.0％ポ
イント上昇し、会長を議長とする会社の割合も1.5％ポイント上昇している。このような社長を取
締役会議長とする会社の割合の低下、社外取締役を議長とする会社の割合の上昇は、取締役会議
長と社長を分離するという、より大きな流れの一部とみることができるだろう。 
次に、どのような会社で社外取締役の取締役会議長選任が進んでいるのかをみるため、2026 年
9 月末時点のプライム市場上場会社について、時価総額ランク別の選任割合を示した（資料4）。

資料 4 時価総額ランク別社外取締役議長選任割合（プライム市場・2026 年 9 月末）

（出所）Bloomberg、東証公表資料データを基に第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

資料 4 からは、時価総額の大きい企業ほど、社外取締役の取締役会議長選任が進んでいるとい
える。なお、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任している会社の社数の割合は7.6％だが、時価総額
では 19％を占める。また、社外取締役が議長となっている会社の39％が時価総額１兆円以上の会
社である。時価総額が大きい企業ほど選任率が高く、大型企業での社外取締役の取締役会議長選
任が相対的に進んでいる。

4．最後に 
ここまで、社外取締役の取締役会議長選任状況について確認してきた。このような社外取締役
の取締役会議長選任はコーポレートガバナンス上、どのような意味を持つのか、最後に私見を述
べたい。 
とりわけ独立社外取締役が議長を務める場合には、独立した立場から取締役会の議論を促し、
監督機能を含む取締役会の実効性向上につながることが期待される。そのためには、経営陣との
適切な連絡・調整や十分な情報提供が重要となる。今回のコーポレートガバナンス・コード改訂
でも、議長や独立社外取締役を支える取締役会事務局（コーポレートセクレタリー等）の機能強

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
化の重要性が示されている。社外取締役を議長に選任する企業には、選任によってどのような効
果を目指すのかを明確にし、それに即した支援体制を整えることが重要であろう。

Document 403: こどもNISAから考える資産形成機会の差 ～政府はどこまで関与すべきか～

Source PDF: all report pdf/こどもNISAから考える資産形成機会の差 ～政府はどこまで関与すべきか～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年10 月 13 日

1/7 
こども NISA から考える資産形成機会の差 
～政府はどこまで関与すべきか～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ 2027 年に始まるこども NISA を題材に、子どもの資産形成を家庭の自助だけに委ねることの
限界と、政府が資産形成にどこまで関与しうるのかを論じる。ドイツを中心とする海外事例
も踏まえ、資産運用政策が「投資の促進」から「投資機会の提供」へ広がる可能性を検討す
る。  
○ こども NISA の資金を出すのは主に親だ。子どもを持つ割合が高い 30 歳代と 40 歳代の世帯
では、同じ年代でも金融資産の保有状況に大きな差がある。J-FLEC の 2025 年調査では、30
歳代の二人以上世帯で金融資産なしが 17.6％を占める一方、1,000 万円以上を保有する世帯
も 25.8％ある。40歳代でもそれぞれ18.8％、37.5％となる。住宅ローンなどの負担もあり、
こどもNISAに回せる資金は同じ年代でも各家庭で相当程度の差が生じると考えられる。 
○ ドイツは2027 年から、6歳から 18歳になるまでの子どもに政府が毎月10ユーロを拠出する
「早期開始年金」を始める予定だ。親が口座を開かなくても政府拠出分を運用する仕組みを
設け、家庭の資金力と親の行動に左右されにくい制度だ。米国、カナダ、英国でも政府によ
る資金拠出が行われてきた。ただ、その方法や成果は一様ではなく、英国のように政府拠出
を終了した例もある。  
○ NISAの普及によって投資しやすい環境は整った一方、実際に投資できる資金や機会には差が
存在する。この差にどう向き合うかが、資産運用政策の次のテーマの一つになるのではない
か。

1. はじめに 
2027 年1月から、少額投資非課税制度（NISA）の対象が子どもにも広がる。通称「こども
NISA」で、18 歳以上だったつみたて投資枠の年齢要件が撤廃され、0～17歳には年間60万円、非
課税保有限度額600万円の枠（注1）が設けられる。投資できるのは「長期・積立・分散」に適し
た投資信託だ。子どもの頃から資産形成をすれば、運用する期間を長く取ることができる。ま
た、運用による利益は非課税になるため、複利効果がより発揮されやすい。 
こどもNISAは、大学進学や成人後のライフイベントなどに伴う資金を準備できるようにするの

### Page 2

2/7 
が狙いだ。一方、実際に投資できるかは各家庭の資金力に左右される。自助を後押しするNISAを
巡り、「NISA貧乏」という言葉がSNSで広がった。NISA貧乏とは、投資に回す資金を捻出するため
に生活費を過度に切り詰め、日々の暮らしに余裕がなくなる状況を指す。この問題が子どもの資
産形成にも及べば、公平性の問題としてこれまで以上に意識されやすい。非課税投資枠を用意し
て投資しやすい環境を整えることと実際に投資できることには隔たりが存在する。この隔たりに
どう向き合うかが、資産運用政策の次のテーマの一つになると考えられる。 
子どもの資産形成支援は海外でも取り組みが進んでおり、ドイツでは 2027年に新しい制度の開
始が予定されている。新制度では6 歳から18 歳までの子どもに毎月10ユーロ（約1,800円）を
政府が拠出する。家庭の資金力にかかわらず、すべての対象者に資産形成の機会を提供する。本
稿では、こどもNISAを概観したうえで、ドイツを中心とする各国の事例から、子どもの資産形成
に政府がどこまで、どのように関与しうるのかを考えたい。

2. こどもNISA誕生の経緯 
こどもNISAは、新NISAのつみたて投資枠を未成年者にも広げる形だ。投資対象は長期・積立・
分散投資に適した投資信託に限る。 
NISAで未成年者を対象に制度を設けるのは、今回で 2度目になる。1回目はジュニアNISAとし
て、2016年に始まった。当時の政府には、NISAを若い世代にも広げるとともに、高齢世代に偏る
金融資産を子や孫の世代へ移したいという問題意識があった（注2）。 
ジュニアNISAは年間の非課税投資枠が80万円で上場株式や投資信託など幅広く投資できた。非
課税期間は当時の一般NISAと同じ最長5年間で、ロールオーバーなどで非課税のまま保有し続け
る仕組みだった。未成年者向けの制度として原則18 歳まで引き出し制限があった。 
ジュニアNISAは、利用実績が乏しいことなどを理由に延長されず、2023年末で新規投資を終了
した（注3） 。終了時点の口座数は約124万口座、累計買付額は1.26 兆円だった。その要因とし
て、制度が時限措置で投資できる期間が限定されていたことと、原則18歳まで資金を払い出せな
かったことがあげられる（注 4） 。子どもの年齢によっては十数年間にわたって資金が拘束される
一方、新規投資できる期間は限られ、非課税期間も原則 5年間とされていた。継続管理勘定など
を通じて18 歳まで非課税で保有できたものの、制度側と利用者側の時間軸がかみ合っていなかっ
たと考えられる。 
こうした課題は、解消・緩和されている。新NISAによって非課税保有期間が無期限となり、制
度も恒久化された。こどもNISAには引き出し制限がかかるものの、12 歳以降は資金を子どものた
めに使い、本人が同意することなどを条件に払い出せるようにする。例えば、進学に伴う入学金
や授業料に充てるといった使い方が想定される。 
使い勝手の改善とともに政府が配慮したのは「格差の固定化につながらないようにする」（注
5）という点だ。年間の非課税投資枠を60 万円、非課税保有限度額を600万円とし、成人向け
NISAより低く設定している。 
もっとも、非課税枠を抑えても、実際にどれだけ投資できるかは家庭が拠出できる資金に左右

### Page 3

3/7 
される。

3. 親世代の金融資産格差 
では、こどもNISAの資金を拠出するとみられる親世代は、どの程度の金融資産を持っているの
か。金融経済教育推進機構（J-FLEC）の2025 年調査で、未成年者の主な親世代と考えられる30
歳代と 40歳代の二人以上世帯をみると、金融資産保有額の中央値は30歳代で 311万円、40 歳代
で500 万円となる。さらに資料1で保有額の分布をみると、30 歳代では金融資産を保有していな
い世帯が17.6％ある一方、1,000万円以上を保有する世帯も25.8％を占める。40歳代でもそれぞ
れ18.8％、37.5％となっており、同じ年代でも金融資産の保有状況には大きな差がある。

資料 1 30・40 歳代の世帯金融資産保有額の分布 
30 歳代                                40 歳代

（注）金融資産には預貯金、株式、投資信託、保険商品などが含まれる。ただし、預貯金のうち日常的な出し入れ・引き落としに備
えている部分は含まない。無回答（30 歳代 2.6％、40 歳代 2.1％）は図示していないため、合計は 100％にならない。 
（出所）J-FLEC「家計の金融行動に関する世論調査 2025 年」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

この世代は住宅ローンなどの負担も大きい。総務省の2025年「家計調査」で二人以上世帯をみ
ると、世帯主が30～39歳では貯蓄現在高1,073万円に対して負債現在高は1,940万円、40～49歳
でも貯蓄1,381万円に対して負債1,483万円だった。 
こどもNISAで年間60 万円という非課税枠の上限を月換算すると5 万円になる。親の収入から継
続的に拠出できる家庭、すでに保有する金融資産から資金を移せる家庭、少額の積み立てにとど
まる家庭などに分かれる。親に加え、祖父母から資金を出してもらえるかどうかによっても差が
生じる。こうした状況を考えると、こどもNISAを使うかどうかを含めて大きな差が生じそうだ。 
長期投資では、こうした投資資金の差がその後の資産形成にも影響する。同じ商品に投資し、

### Page 4

4/7 
同じ収益率を得ても、投資元本が大きければ得られる運用益も大きくなる。子どもの頃から長期
間運用すれば、その差が積み重なっていく。 
前述した通り政府もこの問題を意識し、未成年者の非課税枠を成人より低く設定した。ただ、
これによって抑えられるのは、資金力のある家庭が受ける税制優遇の大きさだ。投資するための
資金が少ない家庭では利用が進みにくく、子どもの資産形成にも差が生じるだろう。家庭の資金
力に左右されずに、子どもが資産形成を始められるよう政府が資金を拠出する制度を予定するの
がドイツだ。

4. ドイツはなぜ政府が初期資金を拠出するのか 
 ドイツ政府は2026年8 月、 「早期開始年金」の法案を閣議決定し、本稿執筆時点では審議中
だ。制度は2027年に開始する予定で、対象となる2020 年生まれの子どもには2026年分から遡っ
て拠出する。6歳から 18歳になるまでの子どもに対し、政府が毎月10ユーロを拠出し、資本市場
で長期運用する（注6） 。年金と名の付く通り、政府拠出分は原則65 歳まで引き出せない超長期の
資産形成支援制度だ。 
制度導入の背景には、子どもの資産形成が家庭環境に左右されているという問題意識がある。
ドイツ財務省は資産形成の機会が親の所得や家庭環境に左右され、その差が老後まで続く可能性
を指摘している（注7） 。家庭が自ら資金を出せるかどうかにかかわらず、政府の拠出によってす
べての対象者が資産形成を始められるようにする。 
ドイツ連邦銀行の調査によれば、投資信託や株式は資産の多い世帯で保有割合が高く、資産の
少ない世帯は預貯金など低リスクの資産が中心となる（注8） 。早期開始年金には、子どもの資産
形成を支えることに加え、長期的に資本市場への参加を広げる狙いもある。 
投資は、口座を開き、商品を選び、実際に資金を投じるという最初の一歩を踏み出すのが難し
い。早期開始年金では、親が投資に積極的かどうかにかかわらず、政府拠出によって子どもの資
産形成が始まる。すべての対象者が少額でも資本市場で運用される資産を持つことになり、最初
のハードルを政府が取り除く意味は大きい。 
さらに、親が口座を開かなかった場合でも、政府拠出分は連邦がまとめて資本市場で運用し、
ドイツ連邦銀行が管理する。政府拠出を受けるための申請も必要なく、親が手続きをしなくても
政府拠出が届く仕組みになっている。 
日本のこどもNISAとドイツの早期開始年金はいずれも、子どもの頃から長期投資を始めるとい
う点では共通する。一方、政府の関わり方には大きな差がある。日本は非課税で運用できる環境
を整えるが、資金の拠出は家庭に委ねている。ドイツは政府が少額とはいえ呼び水としての資金
を拠出する。子どもの資産形成をどこまで家庭に委ね、どこから政府が関与するのかという違い
が表れている。

5. 子どもの資産形成に政府が資金を拠出する世界の動き 
子どもの資産形成への政府による支援をめぐっては、各国が試行錯誤を続けている。米国では

### Page 5

5/7 
2026 年7月、18歳未満の子どもを対象にした「トランプ口座」が始まった。2025～2028年生まれ
の米国民には、連邦政府が1 人 1,000ドル（約16万円）を最初に拠出し、親や祖父母、親の勤務
先の企業などが追加で拠出できる（注9） 。政府が最初の資金を拠出することで、対象となる子ど
もは親が資金を用意できるかどうかにかかわらず、一定の金融資産を持つところから資産形成を
始められる。 
政府による資金拠出の方法は国によって異なる。カナダでは1998年から、教育資金を積み立て
る家庭に対して政府が原則20％を上乗せする仕組みが続いている。2005年には、低所得世帯の子
どもに対し、家庭が資金を拠出しなくても政府が最大2,000カナダドル（約22 万円）を拠出する
「カナダ学習債」も始まった。家庭の積み立てへの上乗せと、家庭からの拠出を必要としない支
援を組み合わせている（注10）。 
英国では2005 年に「チャイルド・トラスト・ファンド」が始まり、原則すべての子どもに政府
が初期資金を拠出し、低所得世帯には上乗せも行った。約630万口座が開設され、政府は累計で
約20億ポンドを拠出した。 
もっとも、政府が資金を拠出した後も家庭が追加できる資金には差が残る。チャイルド・トラ
スト・ファンドは低所得世帯への政府拠出を手厚くした一方、親族などによる追加拠出も認めて
いた。英国財政研究所は、所得の高い家庭ほど追加拠出額が大きくなるため、その子どもの口座
残高も大きくなる可能性を指摘していた。英国ではその後、財政再建の一環として政府拠出を縮
小・廃止した。2011年11月に始まった「ジュニアISA」は非課税で貯蓄・投資できる仕組みを引
き継いだが、資金は家庭が用意する形となった（注11）。

資料 2 子どもの資産形成を支える各国の制度

（出所）各国の公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

各国の経験からは、政府による資金拠出にも様々な方法があることが分かる。一律に資金を拠
出する方法、家庭の積み立てに上乗せする方法、低所得世帯への支援を手厚くする方法などだ。
一方、政府が資金を拠出した後も、家庭が追加できる資金には差が残る。英国のように財政負担
から政府拠出を終了した例もあり、政府拠出の効果や財政面での持続可能性も課題となる。

6. 「投資の促進」から「投資機会の提供」へ 
NISAの普及によって、日本でも投資は身近になった。それでも第3章でみた通り、30歳代と40
歳代でも2 割弱の世帯は金融資産を保有していない。 
この点で、ドイツの早期開始年金は、政府の資金拠出により、家庭の資金力にかかわらず、子
どもの頃から資産形成が始められる仕組みだ。すべての対象者が少額でも資本市場で運用される
資産を持つ意味は小さくない。 
こどもNISAが始まれば、親世代の資金力の差が子どもの資産形成の差としても表れ、資産形成
機会の差がより強く意識されるかもしれない。政府拠出には、財政負担をめぐる課題もある。し
かし、投資が身近になったからこそ、資産形成を始める機会を家庭の資金力や親の行動だけに委
ねない工夫が必要になってくる。資産運用政策の次のテーマとして、自助による「投資の促進」
に加え、資産形成の最初の一歩を支える「投資機会の提供」に向き合うべきだろう。
